Then the following interpolant transformation works for the LCIP:

{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$iRj,\mathcal{R}^{*},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}$}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to83.9169pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 0.83353pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R},\Pi;\Pi^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}^{j\mapsto\Diamond i}}\Sigma;\Sigma^{\prime}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 0.83353pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\ .

The transformations in
\cref
lem:box_gen,lem:diamond_gen also work for the LLIP if assumption (
1
) is strengthened to satisfy
\cref
def:lablyndint(
1^{\prime}
), taking polarity into account (for details see
[
56
]
).

Proof.

We treat
\cref
lem:diamond_gen (
\cref
lem:box_gen is dual). Here we only prove property
2
from
\cref
def:msint. Let
\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}
be an interpretation for
\mathcal{R},\Pi;\Pi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Sigma;\Sigma^{\prime}
. Suppose that
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\not\models\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}^{j\mapsto\Diamond i}
, where multiformulas
\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}
and
\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}^{j\mapsto\Diamond i}
are as in
\cref
ex:mod_trans. Then, for each
1\leq k\leq m
, either
\mathcal{M},\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}i\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}\not\models\Diamond B_{k}
or
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\not\models\mho_{k}
. Thus, for all
v
such that
\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}i\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}Rv
either
\mathcal{M},v\not\models B_{k}
or
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}_{j}^{v}\not\models\mho_{k}

(since
j\notin\mathtt{L}(\mho_{k})
). Consequently,
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}_{j}^{v}\not\models\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}_{j}^{v}\not\models\mho
for all
v
such that
\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}i\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}Rv
. Given that
\mho
is an interpolant of the premise,
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}^{iR\forall}_{j}\models iRj,\mathcal{R}^{*},\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
, which yields
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\models\mathcal{R},\Pi\Rightarrow\Sigma
by the first assumed validity.

Suppose now that that
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\models\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}^{j\mapsto\Diamond i}
. Then, there is some
1\leq k\leq m
such that
\mathcal{M},\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}i\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}\models\Diamond B_{k}
and
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\models\mho_{k}
. From the former, we find a
v
such that
\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}i\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.60022pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-3.60022pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 3.60022pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.94278pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.94278pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.94278pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.67822pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}\kern-2.67822pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{i}\right.$}}\kern 2.67822pt$}}}Rv
, and
\mathcal{M},v\models B_{k}
, and
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}_{j}^{v}\models\mho_{k}
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}_{j}^{v}\models\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}_{j}^{v}\models\mho
for this
v
. Given that
\mho
is an interpolant of the premise, we get
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}^{iR\exists}_{j}\models iRj,\mathcal{R}^{*},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
, which yields the requisite
\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\models\mathcal{R},\Pi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Sigma^{\prime}
by the second assumed validity.
∎

Example 80

(
\Box
- and
\Diamond
-like rules)
.

The split version of
(\mathsf{L}\Box_{r})
is
\Box
-like (resp.
\Diamond

is on the right (resp. left) side of the split. Consequently, the following interpolant transformations work for the LCIP/LLIP w.r.t. any class of models:

iRj,\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho}{j}\mathord{:}{\varphi},\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}

\scriptstyle L(\mathsf{L}\Box_{r})\;

\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}^{j\mapsto\Diamond i}}{i}\mathord{:}{\Box\varphi},\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}

iRj,\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime},{j}\mathord{:}{\varphi}

\scriptstyle R(\mathsf{L}\Box_{r})\;

\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho_{\mathbin{\varowedge}j\mathbin{\varovee}}^{j\mapsto\Box i}}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime},{i}\mathord{:}{\Box\varphi}

where

does and

does not occur in the conclusion.
Rule

\Diamond

\mathcal{M}\mathopen{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}\cdot\mathclose{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 3.33328pt\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-3.33328pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 3.33328pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 2.76108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.76108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.76108pt$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 2.52776pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}\kern-2.52776pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vphantom{\cdot}\right.$}}\kern 2.52776pt$}}}^{iR\forall}_{j}\models
parts in

i
occurring and
j
not occurring in the conclusion, w.r.t. any class
\mathcal{C}
of serial frames, both transformations are valid:

{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 19.57318pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 1.32622pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$iRj,\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}$}}}\kern 1.32622pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(\mathsf{L}{}D)\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to81.9721pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho_{\mathbin{\varovee}j\mathbin{\varowedge}}^{j\mapsto\Diamond i}}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\qquad{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 19.57318pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 1.32622pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$iRj,\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}$}}}\kern 1.32622pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(\mathsf{L}{}D)\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to81.9721pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R},\Gamma;\Gamma^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\mho_{\mathbin{\varowedge}j\mathbin{\varovee}}^{j\mapsto\Box i}}\Delta;\Delta^{\prime}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\ .

Note that the
\Box\theta
and

Example 81
.

Let
\varphi=\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)
and
\psi=\Box(q\vee r)
. In
\cref
fig:lab_ex, we find an interpolant of
\varphi\rightarrow\psi
using the split labelled calculus for logic
DB
(the logic of symmetric serial frames), which has no cut-free sequent calculus.

\scriptstyle\mathit{LR}(\mathsf{Lid}^{*})\;

3R2,2R3,1R2,{2}\mathord{:}{q},{3}\mathord{:}{p},{3}\mathord{:}{\Box q},{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{q},{2}\mathord{:}{r}

\scriptstyle R(\mathsf{L}\vee_{r})\;

3R2,2R3,1R2,{2}\mathord{:}{q},{3}\mathord{:}{p},{3}\mathord{:}{\Box q},{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle L(\mathsf{L}\Box_{l})\;

3R2,2R3,1R2,{3}\mathord{:}{p},{3}\mathord{:}{\Box q},{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}\mathit{B}\;

2R3,1R2,{3}\mathord{:}{p},{3}\mathord{:}{\Box q},{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle L(\mathsf{L}\wedge_{l})\;

2R3,1R2,{3}\mathord{:}{(p\wedge\Box q)},{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle L(\mathsf{L}\Box_{l})\;

2R3,1R2,{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{q}\equiv{3}\mathord{:}{\top}\mathbin{\varowedge}{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}\mathit{D}\;

1R2,{2}\mathord{:}{\Box(p\wedge\Box q)},{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{\Diamond\top}\mathbin{\varowedge}{2}\mathord{:}{q}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle L(\mathsf{L}\Box_{l})\;

1R2,{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{2}\mathord{:}{\Diamond\top}\mathbin{\varowedge}{2}\mathord{:}{q}\equiv{2}\mathord{:}{q}\mathbin{\varovee}{1}\mathord{:}{\bot}}\ ;{2}\mathord{:}{(q\vee r)}

\scriptstyle R(\mathsf{L}\Box_{r})\;

{1}\mathord{:}{\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)};\ \xRightarrow{{1}\mathord{:}{\Box q}\mathbin{\varovee}{1}\mathord{:}{\bot}}\ ;{1}\mathord{:}{\Box(q\vee r)}

Figure 23
:
Labelled interpolant computation in
\cref

Thus,
\mathop{\mathsf{form}}({1}\mathord{:}{\Box q}\mathbin{\varovee}{1}\mathord{:}{\bot})=\Box q\vee\bot
, or equivalently
\Box q
, is a Lyndon interpolant of
\varphi\rightarrow\psi=\Box\Box(p\wedge\Box q)\rightarrow\Box(q\vee r)
in logic
DB
. Here rule
(\mathsf{L}{}D)
was treated as a
\Diamond
-like, with label
3
being
\mathbin{\varovee}\mathbin{\varowedge}
-separated in
{3}\mathord{:}{\top}\mathbin{\varowedge}{2}\mathord{:}{q}\equiv{2}\mathord{:}{q}
. Similarly, label
2
is
\mathbin{\varowedge}\mathbin{\varovee}
-separated in
{2}\mathord{:}{q}\mathbin{\varovee}{1}\mathord{:}{\bot}\equiv{2}\mathord{:}{\Diamond\top}\mathbin{\varowedge}{2}\mathord{:}{q}
. (For this logic,
\Diamond\top
may be dropped due to seriality. In, e.g.,
K
, one would use
{2}\mathord{:}{(\Diamond\top\wedge q)}\mathbin{\varovee}{1}\mathord{:}{\bot}
instead.)
\lipicsEnd

In the next section we see how these techniques lead to powerful interpolation results.

5.3
Interpolation Results for Labelled, Hyper-, and Nested Sequents

The general approach explained in the previous section of defining interpolants by looking at the shape of labelled split rules has two advantages. First, it leads to interpolation results (including Lyndon interpolation) for a range of logics. Second, the techniques can be adjusted to other types of sequents such as nested sequents and hypersequents. In this section we discuss results in both directions and point to relevant literature for more details.

Composing the algorithm for generating cut-free labelled sequent calculi rules
[
67
]
with the algorithm discussed in
\cref
sect:lab_interp for constructing interpolant transformations can yield general theorems as follows:

Theorem 82

(
[
56
]
)
.

The modal logic
\textnormal{{L}}_{\mathcal{C}}
of any class
\mathcal{C}
of frames defined by a set of frame conditions in the Horn-clause form
i_{1}Rj_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge i_{m}Rj_{m}\rightarrow kRl
has the LIP.

Corollary 83
.

Of the frame conditions listed in
[
31
, Sect. 8]
, any combination of seriality, reflexivity, transitivity, symmetry, euclideanity, and shift reflexivity produces a logic with the LIP. This includes all 15 modal logics of the so-called modal cube
[
31
, Sect. 8]
, among them
K
,
T
,
D
,
K4
,
S4
, and
S5
from
\cref
fig:modal-logics.

The range of Kripke-frame classes guaranteeing the LIP can be expanded further, in particular to deal with the convergence restriction
[
31
, Sect. 8]
and
hijk
-convergence
[
31
, Sect. 9]
, yielding logics often named after Scott–Lemon or Geach.
For details we refer to
[
56
]
.

With
\cref
cor:modal_logics we can illustrate that labelled sequent calculi can be more suitable to obtain Lyndon interpolation than ordinary sequent calculi with some form of cut. For example, in
\cref
thm:interpolationSvijf we used Maehara’s method to prove Craig interpolation for logic
S5
by using the analytic cut rule. However, analytic cuts do not preserve polarity, resulting in the inability to obtain Lyndon interpolation.
\Cref
cor:modal_logics, by contrast, establishes the LIP for
S5
using the techniques for labelled sequents.

Now we turn to interpolation results via hypersequents and nested sequents. Nested sequents and hypersequents are closely related to labelled sequents. A labelled sequent
S=\mathcal{R},\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
can be viewed as consisting of ordinary, unlabelled sequents
\{\Gamma_{i}\Rightarrow\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in\mathtt{L}(S)}
where multisets
\Gamma_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\varphi\mid{i}\mathord{:}{\varphi}\in\Gamma\}
and
\Delta_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\varphi\mid{i}\mathord{:}{\varphi}\in\Delta\}
contain (unlabelled) formulas. In this view, each sequent
\Gamma_{i}\Rightarrow\Delta_{i}
is a
sequent component of
S
and relational atoms from
\mathcal{R}
describe the relationships among these sequent components. Relational atoms are expressive enough to represent any (Kripke-like) relationship. If a particular relationship is regular enough, it can be encoded by structural connectives, bypassing the use of explicit labels.

Hypersequents

A
hypersequent
is defined as a finite multiset (alternatively, a set or a list) of sequent components, commonly written as
\Gamma_{1}\Rightarrow\Delta_{1}\mid\dots\mid\Gamma_{n}\Rightarrow\Delta_{n}
. For example, modal logic
S5
admits a hypersequent calculus where hypersequents can be seen as labelled sequents in which
\mathcal{R}
describes a total relation (e.g.,
iRj\in\mathcal{R}
for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
). Although hypersequents predate labelled sequents, having been introduced in
[
65
,
74
,
9
]
, the labelled view on hypersequents means that any interpolant transformation from
\cref
sect:lab_interp can be used whenever its conditions are satisfied. To use the interpolation algorithm, labels have to be assigned to each sequent component of each hypersequent in a proof. One may need to be careful when assigning labels, depending on whether a rule modifies a sequent component or replaces it with a different component. The latter case calls for using a different label, e.g., the proper labelling of Avron’s
(\Rightarrow\Box)
rule from
[
9
]
can be understood as follows:

{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 20.55551pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 3.8889pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$G\mid\Box\Gamma\Rightarrow\varphi$}}}\kern 3.8889pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tiny\Rightarrow\Box\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to58.43178pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$G\mid\Box\Gamma\Rightarrow\Box\varphi$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$G\mid\overbrace{\ \ \ }^{i}\mid\overbrace{\Box\Gamma\Rightarrow\varphi}^{j}$}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to29.52911pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 4.86111pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$G\mid\underbrace{\Box\Gamma\Rightarrow\Box\varphi}_{i}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 4.86111pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\quad\rightsquigarrow\quad{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 20.48615pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 2.64354pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\Pi,{j}\mathord{:}{\Box\Gamma}\Rightarrow\Sigma,{j}\mathord{:}{\varphi}$}}}\kern 2.64354pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tiny\mathsf{L}\Rightarrow\Box\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to73.84866pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\;j\notin X\sqcup\{i\}$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Pi,{i}\mathord{:}{\Box\Gamma}\Rightarrow\Sigma,{i}\mathord{:}{\Box\varphi}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 51.92706pt}}}

where
X=\mathtt{L}(G)
is the set of labels assigned to the side components from
G
.
Consequently,
j
is a label to be removed from the premise interpolant, and the two split versions of this rule are
\Box
-like and
\Diamond
-like, rather than local. See
[
53
]
and
[
56
, Sect. 3]
for more details and interpolant transformations for other rules.
The proof-theoretic methods to prove the LIP directly via hypersequents have been applied to
S5
and modal logic
S4.2
in
[
53
,
56
]
.

Nested Sequents, or Tree-Hypersequents

Another commonly used configuration of sequent components is a tree of sequent components
[
16
,
73
,
57
]
, usually under the names of
nested sequents
or
tree-hypersequents
. Tree structures can be conveniently represented by labels consisting of sequences of integers, so it is no surprise that prefixed tableaux relate to nested sequents the same way as ordinary tableaux do to ordinary sequents
[
27
]
. In this case, the implicit relational atoms are of the form
\sigma R\sigma.n
, where
\sigma
is a sequence of integers and
\sigma.n
is obtained by appending it with integer
n
. All the details can be found in
[
25
]
and
[
56
, Sect. 5]
. Here, we only show an instance of a nested rule whose splits are
\Box
- and
\Diamond
-like rules respectively:

{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 1.11111pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\Pi\{\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,[\varphi]\}$}}}\kern 1.11111pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to66.40274pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Pi\{\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,\Box\varphi\}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\qquad\rightsquigarrow\qquad{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\sigma R\sigma.j,\mathcal{R},\Pi^{\ell},{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Gamma}\Rightarrow{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Delta},{\sigma.j}\mathord{:}{\varphi}$}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to124.89961pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\;\sigma.j\notin\mathcal{R},\Pi^{\ell},{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Gamma}\Rightarrow{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Delta},{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Box\varphi}$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 15.90631pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R},\Pi^{\ell},{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Gamma}\Rightarrow{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Delta},{\sigma}\mathord{:}{\Box\varphi}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 15.90631pt}}}\kern 120.99142pt}}}

where
\Pi^{\ell}
is the labelled translation of the nested context
\Pi\{\>\}
, sequent
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,\Box\varphi
is an ordinary sequent labelled with an integer sequence
\sigma
, and
\mathcal{R}
describes the tree structure of the nested tree of
\Pi\{\>\}
. Here, label
\sigma.j
is the one to be removed from the premise interpolant. The proof-theoretic methods to prove the LIP directly via nested sequents have been applied
[
25
,
56
]
to all 15 logics of the modal cube from
[
31
, Sect. 8]
.

Intermediate Logics

All formalisms so far in
\cref
sect:gener dealt with
{\sf CPC}
-based modal logics.
The method can also be applied to intermediate logics, with intuitionistic implication and negation playing the role of modality in modal logics. In most cases, this requires specialized, restricted types of nested sequents and/or hypersequents (see
[
51
,
52
]
for details). All seven interpolable intermediate logics can be dealt with using one calculus or another. Moreover, the LIP for the Gödel–Dummett logic, the logic of linear intuitionistic Kripke frames, was first demonstrated using this method
[
51
]
.

Uniform interpolation

Hypersequents and nested sequents can also be used to demonstrate the UIP/ULIP. This has been done to reprove the UIP for logics
K
,
D
, and
T
, and to provide the first proof-theoretic proof of the ULIP for logic
K5

[
86
,
85
]
. Similar to the labelled sequent definition of Craig interpolation (
\Cref
def:msint), a labelled version of uniform interpolation is incorporating semantic notions, based on bisimulation quantifiers.

6
Conclusion

This chapter provides methods for proving interpolation results that simultaneously provide algorithms for constructing interpolants based on various (generalized) sequent calculi. For each algorithm, standard interpolant transformations are presented for common rule types, which cover a wide range of logics. As a result, the chapter can be used as a manual: To prove a particular interpolation property for a logic represented by a given calculus, the rules of the calculus should be checked against the common rule types. If each of the calculus rules is covered by one of these types, the interpolation property directly follows. Otherwise, to prove interpolation, it is sufficient to develop interpolant transformations only for the rules not covered by the common types. The proof-theoretic techniques for sequent calculi are further embedded within the framework of universal proof theory, which systematically relates interpolation properties to the existence of well-behaved proof systems.

It is a common misconception that the existence of a cut-free (sequent) calculus for a logic necessarily implies that the logic enjoys interpolation. It may be impossible to provide an interpolant transformation for a particular rule, even for an analytic one. An example of such non-interpolable hypersequent rule for the Gödel–Dummett logic can be found in the proof of
[
51
, Theorem 4.6]
. Note that this logic does enjoy interpolation, both Craig and Lyndon. Thus,
more complex calculi may be necessary despite the existence of a cut-free sequent one.

Related topics

Here we would like to point to some related research directions on proof-theoretic methods of interpolation that fall beyond the scope of this chapter:

•

Interpolation as cut-introduction:
In
[
76
]
, a
proof-relevant
view on Maehara’s method in linear logic is provided in the sense that given a cut-free sequent proof
\pi
of
\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
, there is a formula
\theta
in the common signature of
\varphi
and
\psi
, and there are sequent proofs
\pi_{1}
,
\pi_{2}
for
\varphi\Rightarrow\theta
and
\theta\Rightarrow\psi
, respectively, such that
\pi_{1}
composed with
\pi_{2}
cut-reduces to
\pi
. This leads to the introduction of cuts by synthesizing the interpolant.

•

Interpolation via cyclic and non-wellfounded proofs:

Chapter 11
of this book discusses Maehara-type methods to construct interpolants for fixpoint logics via sequent proofs that may contain infinite branches.

•

Interpolation and proof complexity:
Interpolation has also been extensively studied in proof systems such as resolution and cutting planes [53, 80], where it plays a central role in proof complexity. In particular,
feasible interpolation
investigates when interpolants can be efficiently extracted from proofs, thereby linking short proofs to small interpolating circuits. This connection provides a powerful bridge between proof complexity and circuit complexity, allowing lower bounds on circuit size to be transferred to lower bounds on proof length and enabling the classification of proof systems and the establishment of exponential separations. These themes are discussed in detail in
Chapter 6
of this book.
